2025-06-15T01:40:26.2982575Z 
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2982748Z <pr_title>
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2983253Z Simplify CI: consolidate bloated workflows and add immediate wheel access
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2983768Z </pr_title>
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2983890Z 
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2983977Z <pr_description>
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2984775Z This PR addresses the CI complexity and reliability issues by dramatically simplifying the workflow structure and providing multiple access methods for package installation.
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2985534Z 
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2985614Z ## Problem
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2985935Z The repository had **6 CI workflow files** with significant redundancy:
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2986354Z - `build-artifacts.yaml`
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2986626Z - `build-packages-dev.yaml` 
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2986901Z - `build-packages-prod.yaml`
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2987177Z - `build-packages-staging.yaml`
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2987455Z - `create-release.yaml`
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2987684Z - `tools.yaml`
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2987804Z 
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2988275Z This created maintenance complexity and CI reliability issues, making it difficult to successfully build and access wheel artifacts.
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2988845Z 
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2988925Z ## Solution
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2989032Z 
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2989131Z ### 1. Consolidated CI Workflows
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2989856Z **Reduced from 6 to 3 focused workflows** (50% reduction):
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2990162Z 
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2990574Z - **`build-and-deploy.yaml`**: Unified workflow that builds once and deploys to appropriate environments based on triggers
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2991450Z - **`commit-wheels.yaml`**: Fallback workflow that commits wheels directly to the repository when CI builds succeed
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2992142Z - **`tools.yaml`**: Unchanged, handles build tools and container management
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2992455Z 
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2992570Z ### 2. Multiple Installation Methods
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2992861Z Users can now install packages via:
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2993040Z 
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2993134Z **One-line installer:**
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2993347Z ```bash
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2993844Z curl -sSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/install.sh | bash -s -- pandas
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2994404Z ```
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2994501Z 
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2994597Z **Direct wheel download:**
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2994823Z ```bash
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2995465Z curl -L https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/wheels/pandas-1.5.0-cp310-cp310-linux_armv7l.whl -o /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2996201Z pip install /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2996422Z ```
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2996519Z 
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2996663Z **CI artifacts and releases** (when CI runs successfully)
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2996911Z 
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2997001Z ### 3. Immediate Package Access
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2997595Z Added pre-built wheel files directly to the repository in the `wheels/` directory, so packages are immediately available even if CI fails.
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2998116Z 
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2998192Z ## Key Benefits
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2998444Z - **Simplified maintenance**: 50% fewer workflow files
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2998935Z - **Eliminated redundancy**: Single build logic instead of duplicated across multiple files
2025-06-15T01:40:26.2999452Z - **Improved reliability**: Multiple fallback access methods
2025-06-15T01:40:26.3000134Z - **Better user experience**: Immediate access to packages via curl/pip as requested
2025-06-15T01:40:26.3000677Z - **Clear documentation**: Added `CI-SIMPLIFICATION.md` explaining the changes
2025-06-15T01:40:26.3000995Z 
2025-06-15T01:40:26.3001367Z The solution directly addresses the "bloated" CI concern while ensuring reliable package access through multiple channels.
2025-06-15T01:40:26.3001847Z 
2025-06-15T01:40:26.3001929Z Fixes #11.
2025-06-15T01:40:26.3002211Z 
2025-06-15T01:40:26.3002215Z 
2025-06-15T01:40:26.3002300Z </pr_description>

2025-06-15T01:40:45.4810289Z                 - paragraph [ref=e642]:
2025-06-15T01:40:45.4811012Z                   - text: OAuth clients provide a framework for delegated and scoped access to the
2025-06-15T01:40:45.4811821Z                   - link "Tailscale API" [ref=e643] [cursor=pointer]:
2025-06-15T01:40:45.4812371Z                     - /url: /kb/1101/api
2025-06-15T01:40:45.4814041Z                   - text: . An OAuth client creates access tokens for scoped API access, instead of using a fully-permitted access token which expires after 90 days (or less if the fully-permitted access token has a shorter expiry). OAuth clients support the
2025-06-15T01:40:45.4815916Z                   - link "principle of least privilege" [ref=e644] [cursor=pointer]:
2025-06-15T01:40:45.4816633Z                     - /url: /learn/principle-of-least-privilege
2025-06-15T01:40:45.4818024Z                   - text: by allowing fine-grained control on the access granted to the client using scopes, unlike a fully-permitted access token which grants full access to the Tailscale API.
2025-06-15T01:40:45.4819398Z                 - heading "How it works" [level=2] [ref=e645]:
2025-06-15T01:40:45.4820130Z                   - link "How it works" [ref=e646] [cursor=pointer]:
2025-06-15T01:40:45.4820687Z                     - /url: "#how-it-works"
2025-06-15T01:40:45.4821237Z                     - generic [ref=e647] [cursor=pointer]: How it works
2025-06-15T01:40:45.4822057Z                     - img [ref=e649] [cursor=pointer]
2025-06-15T01:40:45.4822567Z                 - paragraph [ref=e651]:
2025-06-15T01:40:45.4823105Z                   - text: You create an OAuth client that defines the
2025-06-15T01:40:45.4823705Z                   - link "scopes" [ref=e652] [cursor=pointer]:
2025-06-15T01:40:45.4824229Z                     - /url: "#scopes"
2025-06-15T01:40:45.4824961Z                   - text: to allow when your client application uses the Tailscale API. An example scope is
2025-06-15T01:40:45.4825975Z                   - code [ref=e653]: dns:read
2025-06-15T01:40:45.4826750Z                   - text: ", which grants read-only access to a tailnet's DNS settings. Another example is the"
2025-06-15T01:40:45.4827544Z                   - code [ref=e654]: devices:core
2025-06-15T01:40:45.4828664Z                   - text: scope, which grants access to read and write the list of devices in the tailnet, authorize or remove machines, and manipulate tags on devices.
2025-06-15T01:40:45.4829937Z                 - paragraph [ref=e655]:
2025-06-15T01:40:45.4830960Z                   - text: Scopes are restricted to only the necessary Tailscale API endpoints needed for an operation. For example, tokens with the
2025-06-15T01:40:45.4832001Z                   - code [ref=e656]: dns:read
2025-06-15T01:40:45.4832642Z                   - text: "scope can access only the following Tailscale API endpoints:"

2025-06-15T01:40:45.4839093Z                 - paragraph [ref=e666]:
2025-06-15T01:40:45.4840992Z                   - text: An OAuth client consists of a client ID and a client secret. When you create an OAuth client, Tailscale creates these for you. Within your client application, use the client ID and client secret to request an API access token from the
2025-06-15T01:40:45.4842982Z                   - link "Tailscale OAuth token endpoint" [ref=e667] [cursor=pointer]:
2025-06-15T01:40:45.4843702Z                     - /url: "#tailscale-oauth-token-endpoint"
2025-06-15T01:40:45.4845040Z                   - text: . You use the access token to make calls to the Tailscale API. The access token grants permission only for the scopes that were defined when you created the OAuth client.
2025-06-15T01:40:45.4847319Z                 - paragraph [ref=e668]: An API access token expires after one hour. For continuous access, shortly before an API access token expires, request a new API access token from the Tailscale OAuth token endpoint.
2025-06-15T01:40:45.4849397Z                 - paragraph [ref=e669]: OAuth client libraries in popular programming languages can handle the API access token generation and renewal.
